Page-fit teaching keys

Return to symbols

These keys explain meaning and revised dimensions. They are outside the graphic. Physical tactile and learner evaluation remains pending.

Ant insect

A one-node wingless worker-ant dorsal example retains a continuous head/neck/mesosoma/stalk/petiole/stalk/gaster contour, two eyes, two elbowed antennae, paired anterior mandibles and six jointed legs attached to the mesosoma. The head remains left; animal-left is displayed lower. Eyes, mandibular apertures and neck/stalk widths are enlarged independently for page size. No callout is present in the whole-ant meaning. This is one chosen worker form, not a universal ant species or node count; mesosoma includes thorax and propodeum. External object-name and viewpoint key required; physical tactile and learner evaluation remain pending. External accessible title and explanation are required; no lettering is embedded.

This is a remade page-fit diagram. Original dimensions and earlier review observations do not apply. Physical tactile and learner evaluation remains pending.

Bee animal

The selected adult worker-bee plan retains the joined head, thorax and broad abdomen; two enlarged compound-eye outlines; two head antennae; six walking legs in three thorax-rooted pairs; and four thorax-rooted wing contours. The source triangular membrane convention remains, without invented venation, stripes, stinger or ant-like abdominal petiole. Appendage angles and eyes are independently enlarged or moved to maintain page-size separation. Two-segment legs are diagram carriers, not anatomical segment counts. External object-name and viewpoint key required; physical tactile and learner evaluation remain pending. External accessible title and explanation are required; no lettering is embedded.

This is a remade page-fit diagram. Original dimensions and earlier review observations do not apply. Physical tactile and learner evaluation remains pending.

Boy young person

The named fictional young-person example retains the source coat, mittens, trousers, boots, hair boundary and one lowered/one extended arm. The source left-right orientation is retained. The page geometry uses 70% source contour dimensions with unchanged 1.4 mm physical lines and newly placed Index dots; the hair cap is independently deepened to retain a usable closed region. No facial, bodily or clothing trait is asserted to identify gender. The boy/girl identity is supplied by the external character key, not inferred from the mirrored pose. No new fill-height meaning is added. External object-name and viewpoint key required; physical tactile and learner evaluation remain pending. External accessible title and explanation are required; no lettering is embedded.

This is a remade page-fit diagram. Original dimensions and earlier review observations do not apply. Physical tactile and learner evaluation remains pending.

Butterfly animal

The dorsal spread-wing butterfly retains four wing regions, joined head/thorax/abdomen and two clubbed antennae. The narrow abdomen and antenna clubs are independently widened; wing attachment positions remain exact on the thorax contour. Legs remain occluded as in the source. Enlarged outlined clubs replace tiny solid dots. This is a generic monarch-informed recognition example, not complete species or sex identification. External object-name and viewpoint key required; physical tactile and learner evaluation remain pending. External accessible title and explanation are required; no lettering is embedded.

This is a remade page-fit diagram. Original dimensions and earlier review observations do not apply. Physical tactile and learner evaluation remains pending.

Cat animal

The side-view cat retains pointed ears, a raised curved tail, four separately readable legs and one near-eye marker. Legs, feet and tail thickness are re-laid out for page-size touch. The small muzzle fold is omitted; the eye marker is enlarged and outlined rather than a tiny solid dot. This is a generic recognition outline, not a breed or anatomical scale model. External object-name and viewpoint key required; physical tactile and learner evaluation remain pending. External accessible title and explanation are required; no lettering is embedded.

This is a remade page-fit diagram. Original dimensions and earlier review observations do not apply. Physical tactile and learner evaluation remains pending.

Cow animal

The cow side outline retains the broad trunk, four legs, long hanging tail with tuft, single visible ear and projecting muzzle. Leg and tail widths are enlarged independently; tiny hoof clefts, nostril and short muzzle fold are omitted. The near-eye is an enlarged outlined position marker. No horns or udder absent from the source are invented. External object-name and viewpoint key required; physical tactile and learner evaluation remain pending. External accessible title and explanation are required; no lettering is embedded.

This is a remade page-fit diagram. Original dimensions and earlier review observations do not apply. Physical tactile and learner evaluation remains pending.

Doctor medical role

The complete source human/equipment composition is rebuilt with fixed physical 1.4 mm strokes and freshly placed Index dots. The stethoscope yoke is shortened vertically and its tube lengthened, with chestpiece moved 1 source mm upwards to retain body clearance. Clothing, hand, skin/hair and footwear domains are retained. Hair caps, where present, are independently deepened. ViewPlus retains the original #555555 line-height role without invented shaded areas. This is a keyed role/context example, not an assertion that a generic figure or isolated tool universally identifies an occupation or action. External object-name and viewpoint key required; physical tactile and learner evaluation remain pending. External accessible title and explanation are required; no lettering is embedded.

This is a remade page-fit diagram. Original dimensions and earlier review observations do not apply. Physical tactile and learner evaluation remains pending.

Dog animal

The generic dog retains a raised tail, four separate legs, broad muzzle and one hanging near-ear. The hanging ear, leg spacing and feet are broadened independently. Tiny nose and mouth marks are omitted; one enlarged outlined near-eye marker remains. This is recognition geometry, not exact animal proportions. External object-name and viewpoint key required; physical tactile and learner evaluation remain pending. External accessible title and explanation are required; no lettering is embedded.

This is a remade page-fit diagram. Original dimensions and earlier review observations do not apply. Physical tactile and learner evaluation remains pending.

Fin areas and spaces are independently enlarged rather than uniformly reduced; the thin snout remains part of the continuous contour. This is the source generic fin arrangement, not a species identification or comparative anatomical scale. Hairbrush object

The horizontal brush retains six representative bristle projections, an elongated rounded head and attached right handle. The six former very thin outlined bristles are deliberately widened to 8 mm and separated by 6 mm centreline gaps; this keeps the material bodies open and traceable. Head and handle are independently widened and their join remains continuous. Bristle count is a selected recognition cue, not a literal product specification. ViewPlus grey stays within the single material silhouette. External object-name and viewpoint key required; physical tactile and learner evaluation remain pending. External accessible title and explanation are required; no lettering is embedded.

This is a remade page-fit diagram. Original dimensions and earlier review observations do not apply. Physical tactile and learner evaluation remains pending.

Jacket garment

The front-view jacket retains two long sleeves, its closed angular hood outline, V-shaped hood/neck seam and continuous centre opening line. The joined source edges are reconstructed as semantic paths with the original physical stroke width and new Index samples. No pockets, buttons, face or body are introduced. External object-name and viewpoint key required; physical tactile and learner evaluation remain pending. External accessible title and explanation are required; no lettering is embedded.

Keyboard device

All forty upper key positions in four rows of ten, plus a wide lower spacebar and two lower side keys, remain inside the bevelled keyboard case. Keys are re-laid out as 10 by 8.2 mm outlines with at least 3.1 mm clear gaps. The 43-position source is a simplified keyboard recognition layout, not a full QWERTY, language-specific, accessibility or functional key map. No lettering or braille is printed. External object-name and viewpoint key required; physical tactile and learner evaluation remain pending. External accessible title and explanation are required; no lettering is embedded.

This is a remade page-fit diagram. Original dimensions and earlier review observations do not apply. Physical tactile and learner evaluation remains pending.

Knife utensil

The blunt table-knife blade, narrowed transition and rounded handle remain one closed outline. The dense source polygon is replaced by a small number of editable curves; no sharp point, serrations or separate blade seam is invented. Object recognition requires its external identity key; this is a teaching outline, not a manufacturing drawing. Physical device and learner evaluation remains pending. External accessible title and explanation are required; no lettering is embedded.

Grey ViewPlus mane/body shading is retained; no whiskers or anatomical detail are added. External object-name and viewpoint key required; physical tactile and learner evaluation remain pending. Monkey animal

The quadrupedal monkey retains its head/muzzle, one visible ear and eye, long leftward tail and four separate limbs. The material outline is expanded outward by 2 mm, while the narrow front-leg opening is widened and the far foreleg is shifted left first to keep it open. The eye and ear are enlarged; tiny nostril and mouth-line details are omitted. The selected external recognition view is not a species-specific anatomical or proportional model. Original grey ViewPlus body shading remains. External object-name and viewpoint key required; physical tactile and learner evaluation remain pending. External accessible title and explanation are required; no lettering is embedded.

Stripe bodies are independently widened by a 1.7 mm outward offset and the muzzle/nose are enlarged for clear tactile interiors. These seven marks are a recognitional pattern, not a universal biological stripe count. The original grey ViewPlus head material is retained. Acoustic guitar object example

The acoustic guitar retains six separate single-course strings, six tuning buttons, one nut, circular soundhole and opaque bridge. The neck is greatly widened and the body shortened independently to preserve all six 8 mm-spaced strings and 9 mm tuning-button glyphs. Strings cross the white soundhole and end at the bridge; this is a tactile teaching layout, not realistic instrument proportions or a maker replica. External object-name and viewpoint key required; physical tactile and learner evaluation remain pending. External accessible title and explanation are required; no lettering is embedded.

This is a remade page-fit diagram. Original dimensions and earlier review observations do not apply. Physical tactile and learner evaluation remains pending.

Actor role context

The actor remains within a separately resized stage header, two side curtain bands and a shared floor. The source tunic, extended hand, lowered hand, head/neck, two exposed legs and two shoes remain. The figure contours are independently rebuilt at 58% source coordinate dimensions with original 1.4 mm strokes; the stage is narrowed independently instead of shrinking the person with it. The lowered hand and forearm are moved 2 mm outward, with the left curtain moved outward to preserve clearance. Shoes keep their actual floor contacts. The theatrical setting plus external role key supplies context; generic clothing or gesture alone does not identify an actor. External object-name and viewpoint key required; physical tactile and learner evaluation remain pending. External accessible title and explanation are required; no lettering is embedded.

This is a remade page-fit diagram. Original dimensions and earlier review observations do not apply. Physical tactile and learner evaluation remains pending.

Adult mandibular dental arch

The selected complete adult lower exploded arch retains all sixteen crown outlines, including third molars: per side two incisors, one canine, two premolars and three molars. Source crown class outlines and patient-right on page-left are retained. The lower arch has incisors at the bottom and posterior teeth at the top. Incisors are enlarged independently and gaps expanded; this is an illustrative class/position inventory, not a clinical shape, root or cusp model and not universal adult dentition. External object-name and viewpoint key required; physical tactile and learner evaluation remain pending. External accessible title and explanation are required; no lettering is embedded.

Afghanistan outline map

North-up Afghanistan retains its main territory and connected eastward Wakhan corridor. Fine boundary vertices are generalized at 1.2 mm and the upper/lower Wakhan sides are independently separated by an extra 8 mm overall, so it remains a connected area rather than a merged line. This deliberately exaggerated corridor is not a legal boundary, accurate area comparison or navigational map. Grey ViewPlus marks mapped land only. External object-name and viewpoint key required; physical tactile and learner evaluation remain pending. External accessible title and explanation are required; no lettering is embedded.

This is a remade page-fit diagram. Original dimensions and earlier review observations do not apply. Physical tactile and learner evaluation remains pending.

Airplane external example

The selected oblique propeller-aircraft view retains fuselage, two main wings, vertical fin, both tailplanes, circular nose hub and two propeller blades. Source-part boundaries and attachment relationships remain, while the far tailplane moves 3 mm forward to clear the fin, and blade tips, widths and hub attachment angles are re-laid out independently to preserve their tactile regions. The hub is 9.6 mm across. This is an aircraft recognition example, not a measured planform, aerodynamic diagram or asserted real aircraft model. External object-name and viewpoint key required; physical tactile and learner evaluation remain pending. External accessible title and explanation are required; no lettering is embedded.

Alaska flag layout context

Seven smaller five-point Big Dipper stars and one larger Polaris star remain, without connecting lines. The complete relative star-centre arrangement is retained with a uniform 1.05 coordinate factor; small stars are enlarged to 10.2 mm radius and Polaris keeps the 10:7 size ratio. The surrounding field changes from 240:165 to 194:146 to remove excess margins. This is an explicit accessible layout adaptation, not cloth proportions or an astronomical chart. The page-fit artwork is rotated clockwise: the hoist is at the top. Turn the page anticlockwise for the conventional horizontal reading orientation. Black outlines and dots do not intrinsically encode country or colour; use the accessible external identity/colour key. This is a teaching layout, not a certified statutory or textile drawing. External accessible title and explanation are required; no lettering is embedded.

This is a remade page-fit diagram. Original dimensions and earlier review observations do not apply. Physical tactile and learner evaluation remains pending.

Aluminum neutral electron shell model

Neutral aluminum counting model: occupied-shell counts from inner to outer are 2, 8, 3; total 13 electrons. The central undifferentiated nuclear reference carries no proton/neutron or isotope count. Even spacing and guide shape are introduced teaching conventions, not actual orbits, measured particle positions, atomic scale or a probability distribution. An external title identifies the element.

This is a remade page-fit diagram. Original dimensions and earlier review observations do not apply. Physical tactile and learner evaluation remains pending.

Andromeda constellation pattern model

The selected north-up star pattern cannot retain all source positions, 8 mm star markers and every connecting guide at least 12.7 mm within 170 x 200 mm. Retaining those features requires at least 206.1 x 180.7 mm of artwork at the guide-length bound. A page-fit rendering would require omitting named guide connections or distorting relative star positions; the original-size version is retained for a fold-out or later multi-panel treatment. Source ICRS J1991.25 positions and introduced guide edges are not a current observing chart or physical connections. Markers do not encode star brightness or size.

Oversized exception: not suitable for automatic one-page fitting or shrinking.

Aneroid barometer dial example

Unnumbered dial example retains a circular bezel, thirteen ordered radial tick marks, a single upper-right pointer and pivot outline. Ticks are independently lengthened to 13 mm, separated from the bezel, and pivot enlarged to 9 mm diameter. The pointer keeps its source orientation midway between the neighbouring upper-right marks. No numeric pressure, calibration, weather forecast or additional pointer is asserted. External object-name and viewpoint key required; physical tactile and learner evaluation remain pending. External accessible title and explanation are required; no lettering is embedded.

This is a remade page-fit diagram. Original dimensions and earlier review observations do not apply. Physical tactile and learner evaluation remains pending.

Earth tropic polar circle model

The selected equatorial orthographic projection fixes the polar-circle chords at plus/minus 66.6 degrees. A polar-cap region has maximum inscribed diameter R*(1-sin(66.6 degrees)); after the retained 1.4 mm stroke it must be at least 6.35 mm. This requires a limb diameter including stroke of 189.860 mm, exceeding 170 mm. Moving the polar circles inward would distort this exact projection. The original 200 by 200 mm files are retained byte-identically as an exception. Their 140 mm limb also does NOT meet the new 6.35 mm polar-cap clearance requirement; this retention is not a nominal-size approval. Use an enlarged or separately explained multi-page version for that requirement. The selected five chords are Arctic circle, Tropic of Cancer, equator, Tropic of Capricorn and Antarctic circle.

Do not shrink or silently displace latitude chords; original polar caps fall below the requested new paper-region minimum.

This preserves the source sparse brace selection, not the complete tower lattice or a measured elevation. Eight phase lunar sequence

All eight phases keep the clockwise order from the top: new, waxing crescent, first quarter, waxing gibbous, full, waning gibbous, third quarter, waning crescent. One 13 mm vertical stroke now marks the illuminated part, replacing two horizontal hatches; new has none. Crescent and gibbous terminators are deliberately widened toward the middle so the stroke and gaps fit. This teaches phase categories and illumination side, not exact illuminated fractions or 45-degree phase samples. Terminators still stop before the outer limb. Waxing is illuminated on the right and waning on the left in the retained northern upright convention. The sequence repeats one Moon; phases are not caused by Earth shadow. External accessible title and explanation are required; no lettering is embedded.

Five lagrange point plan

The source fixed-mass-ratio (mu=0.001) horizontal-axis plan places L1 and the secondary primary too close for distinct minimum-size semantic glyphs on one page. Retaining source positions and glyph orientations yields at most 5.35 mm between their centres at 170 mm width; even optimistic 6.35 mm-wide glyphs and a 3 mm gap need 9.35 mm. The original-size plan is retained for a fold-out or a separately designed multi-panel explanation. Circle glyphs denote physical primaries; diamonds denote equilibrium locations, not extra bodies. Triangular guides are geometric constructions, not trajectories.
